- original:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Askia, leader of Songhai (Player 6).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Songhai
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Mandekalu Cavalry, Replacing Knight (Requires technology: Chivalry)
        - Unique Unit: Sofa, Replacing Crossbowman (Requires technology: Machinery)
        - Unique Building: Tabya, Replacing Stone Works (Requires technology: Construction)
        - Unique Building: Gumey, Replacing Caravansary (Requires technology: Currency)
        - Civilization Ability: Triple Gold from destroying Encampments and plundering Cities. Land Units gain the Amphibious and War Canoes Promotions.
      - Leader: Askia
      - PlayerID: 6
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Radar: Allows you to build the Heavy Bomber, a deadly air Unit capable of delivering death from afar, and the Fighter, an air Unit designed to wrest control of the skies from enemy aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Advanced Ballistics, Mobile Tactics
      - Nuclear Fission: Allows you to build the Bazooka, the most powerful ranged Unit and Atomic Bombs, the first nuclear weapon in the game (note: you must first have completed the Manhattan Project and have Uranium available). Also allows you to build the Nuclear Plant, a building which requires Uranium and increases the Production of a City.
        Completing it would unlock: Satellites, Advanced Ballistics
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      - Electronics: Allows you to build the Police Station, increasing City security level.
        Completing it would unlock: Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
      
      ## Policies
      - Dictatorship of the Proletariat (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Dictatorship of the Proletariat: +7 Food, Science, Gold, and Culture per city.
      - Academy of Sciences (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Academy of Sciences: -2 Unhappiness from Illiteracy in all Cities, +100% Production towards Research Labs, and receive 5 Free Research Labs in your Empire. Research Labs generate an additional +2 Science.
      - Five-Year Plan (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Five-Year Plan: +20% Production towards Buildings in all Cities. +3 Production for every Mine, Quarry, Lumber Mill, Oil Well, and Unique Improvement.
      - Resettlement (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Resettlement: New Cities have +3 Population. All Cities gain +2 Citizens immediately. No Partisans from razing enemy Cities.
      - Martial Law (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * No Gold Maintenance from Garrisons.
        - * +1 Happiness and +4 Culture for each Garrison.
        - * -20% Yield penalties of Puppeted Cities.
      - Nationalization (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Nationalization: Each Corporate Office counts as a Franchise. Foreign Franchises no longer benefit your Corporation, you can no longer construct Franchises in foreign Cities (except Vassals), and foreign Corporations can no longer set up Franchises in your Cities (except Masters). +2 Science from all Corporation Offices.
      - Exploitation (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Food and +1 Production from Farms, Camps, and Plantations.
        - * +1 Production and Science from Coasts, Lakes, and Oceans.
      - Cultural Revolution (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Cultural Revolution: +25% Tourism to civilizations with less Happiness. +1 Happiness from Factories.
      - Great Leap Forward (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Great Leap Forward: Receive a free Technology. Spies gain +100% Science. +1 Science in Cities for every 3 non-Specialist Citizens.
      - Workers' Faculties (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Worker Faculties: Factories increase City Science output by 10%. +100% Production towards Factories, and receive 5 Free Factories in your Empire.
      - People's Army (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): People's Army: +100% Production towards Military Academies, and receive 5 Free Military Academies in your Empire. Public Schools produce +5 Culture and +2 Happiness.
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.
      - Hero of the People (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Hero of the People: Great Person rate increases by 25%. A Great Person of your choice appears near your Capital.
      - Double Agents (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Double Agents: Receive 200 Spy Points. Gain 125 Science when you identify a foreign Spy, scaling with Era.
      - Communism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Communism: Gold cost of investing in buildings reduced by 25%. +20% Production when building Wonders.
      - Free Thought (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -5 Unhappiness from Religious Unrest in all Cities.
        - * +25% Instant Yields from Great Scientists.
        - * Earn Great Scientists +33% faster.
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Serfdom (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Culture and Faith in Cities for every 4 non-Specialist Citizens.
        - * +2 Production and +1 Gold from Pastures.
      - Organized Religion (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +25% Pressure from owned Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 10 Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +2 Culture from Shrines and Temples.
      - Divine Right (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +33% Yields from Internal Trade Routes.
        - * Completing an Internal Trade Route triggers a Historic Event as if completing an International Trade Route.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Progress (New Branch):
        - Progress is best for civilizations which desire robust infrastructure and scientific advancement.
        - Adopting Progress grants:
        - * Receive 20 Science when a Citizen is born in your Capital and 15 Science for every Citizen already in your Capital, both scaling with Era.
        - * Receive 15 Culture when a Technology is researched, scaling with Era, and for every Technology already unlocked (not scaling with Era).
        - Each Progress policy unlocked grants:
        - * Gain an additional 15 Culture when a Technology is researched, scaling with Era.
        - Adopting all policies in Progress grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Forbidden Palace.
        - * Receive 25 Gold when a Citizen is born in any city, scaling with Era.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Writers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 10
        - WonderCompetitiveness: 5
        - MinorCivCompetitiveness: 10
        - Boldness: 10

        - HostileBias: 10

        - DenounceWillingness: 10
        - Forgiveness: 1
        - Meanness: 10
        - Neediness: 1
        - Chattiness: 1
        - DeceptiveBias: 5
        - Rationale: We are focusing on maintaining city-state alliances for resources and World Congress votes, not hostility. Lowering hostile bias ensures our AI does not unnecessarily antagonize city-states while we pursue domination.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: Radar
        - Rationale: Radar unlocks Heavy Bombers and Fighters, providing critical air superiority and bombing capability to complement our upcoming nuclear arsenal. This will allow us to shatter Chinese defenses and achieve air dominance in the upcoming war.
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: Industry (New Branch)
        - Rationale: Industry's economic bonuses will strengthen our war economy, enabling faster unit purchases and infrastructure development.
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Jerusalem
        - Public: -30
        - Private: -30
        - Rationale: Jerusalem is at peace with us but remains Japan's ally and could redeclare. Keeping moderate hostility maintains caution without forcing immediate war, allowing us to focus on China and Japan. No change needed.
        - UpdatedTurn: 146
        
        ### Bunkeya
        - Public: -30
        - Private: -30
        - Rationale: Jerusalem is at peace with us but remains Japan's ally and could redeclare. Keeping moderate hostility maintains caution without forcing immediate war, allowing us to focus on China and Japan. No change.
        - UpdatedTurn: 242
        
        ### The Aztecs
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: The Aztecs are our current active enemy; we are winning (war score 32%) and capturing their cities (Tzintzuntzen is razing). Maintain maximum hostility to ensure we eliminate them completely before turning to China. No change needed.
        - UpdatedTurn: 344
        
        ### Japan
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Japan is on the brink of collapse (only 3 cities left, war score 26%). We must finish them quickly to free our forces for China and The Aztecs. No peace until they are eliminated.
        - UpdatedTurn: 344
        
        ### Egypt
        - Public: 80
        - Private: 80
        - Rationale: Egypt remains our most critical ally, with a massive military (12530 strength) and shared Order ideology. They are actively helping capture Japanese cities and will be crucial in the upcoming war against China (they have a defensive pact with China, but we expect they will join us when we declare). Maintaining maximum positive relations ensures continued open borders, trade, and military cooperation. We must keep Egypt firmly on our side to avoid a two-front war with both China and Egypt.
        - UpdatedTurn: 357
        
        ### Venice
        - Public: 80
        - Private: 80
        - Rationale: Venice is our defensive pact ally and a valuable trade partner. They provide economic benefits and secure our western flank. Keeping relations at maximum ensures the pact remains active and they do not betray us. Venice is also friendly with China, but our defensive pact with them should prevent them from joining China against us. High relations reduce the risk of them breaking the pact or denouncing us.
        - UpdatedTurn: 357
        
        ### India
        - Public: 60
        - Private: 60
        - Rationale: India is a friendly trade partner and also at war with The Aztecs, providing a useful distraction. While they have ideological differences (Freedom vs. our Order), they are not an immediate threat and can serve as a buffer. We keep relations positive to maintain trade and avoid another enemy, but not so high as to distract from our primary conquest goals. They are also a major diplomatic contender, but our focus is military domination.
        - UpdatedTurn: 357
        
        ### China
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: China is our primary future target; peace treaty expires in 6 turns. They have a defensive pact with The Maya and a strong bomber force. Maximum hostility ensures our AI does not seek peace and continues military buildup. We must be ready to strike immediately after finishing The Aztecs and Japan. China's territory is large (10 cities) and their military is strong (3703 strength), so we need to maintain full war focus.
        - UpdatedTurn: 357
        
        ### The Maya
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: The Maya are in a defensive pact with China and will join any war we start against China. To ensure our AI remains fully committed to fighting them and does not seek peace, we set maximum hostility. They are currently unhappy and at war with The Aztecs, but they will become a co-belligerent when we attack China. We must be prepared to fight them simultaneously.
        - UpdatedTurn: 358
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: We are on the brink of nuclear warfare and preparing for a massive offensive against China and The Maya. Increasing UseNuke and Nuke flavors ensures aggressive nuclear strike planning and arsenal buildup. Air and Antiair at maximum to dominate the skies and counter China's bomber force. All other military flavors remain high to sustain our multi-front war and rapid conquest.
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest
        
        ### Flavors

        - Defense: 80
        - Mobilization: 100
        - CityDefense: 90
        - MilitaryTraining: 100
        - Recon: 10

        ## DiplomaticVictory
        - VotesNeeded: 43
        - Status: World Congress - Voting in 9 turns
        - ActiveResolutions:
          - Casus Belli:
            - Description: The world recognizes the need for justifiable war to resolve diplomatic disputes.
              While this resolution is active, unit maintenance costs are lowered by 15% Gold. 0.5x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 2x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Cultural Heritage Sites:
            - Description: Each World Wonder provides +2 Culture to its city, and each Great Work provides +1 Science to its city.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Indian Sphere of Influence: Dorestad:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 366
          - World Science Initiative:
            - Description: Increases up to 33% generation of Great Scientists, Engineers, and Merchants. Decreases up to 33% generation of Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians.
              Civilizations with Scores from Technologies and Future Tech below the global median will receive a boost to Food and Science up to 33%.
              Details: The boosts are proportional to the difference between the score of the Civilization and the leader.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Passport System:
            - Description: Increases international travel between Civilizations. Increases the Tourism modifier for all Civilizations by 50%.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Flight.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Historical Landmarks:
            - Description: Great Person tile improvements provide +1 to Food, Production, and Gold when worked, and Landmarks built by Archaeologists provide +1 Science, Culture, and Faith when worked.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Archaeology.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Egyptian Sphere of Influence: Abernethy:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - World Religion: Shinto:
            - Description: Designate a religion as the official World Religion. Civilizations who have a majority of the cities in their empire following the religion get an additional Delegate. The founder of the religion and/or the controller of its Holy City receives additional votes based on the number of Civilizations following the religion. The religion spreads 25% faster, and its Holy City receives +50% Tourism. There can only be one World Religion at a time.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Scholars In Residence:
            - Description: Civilizations have -20% Research cost for Technologies already discovered by another Civilization. Additional -2% for every City-State Ally, up to -50%.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Indian Sphere of Influence: Sokoto:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Venetian Sphere of Influence: Kwa-Bulawayo:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 344
          - Indian Sphere of Influence: Riga:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Venetian Sphere of Influence: Oslo:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Sanction: The Aztecs:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: 355
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: Cold War:
            - Description: Ideological differences have pushed the world into a cold war, and economic and political factions are forming based on ideology alone.
              While this resolution is active, players may only make Trade Routes with civilizations that follow the same ideology, or with allied City-States. Furthermore, Influence levels with allied City-States do not decline.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Atomic Theory.
            - Proposer: Egypt
          - Enact: Indian Sphere of Influence: La Venta:

- replay:
  - system:
    - You are the leader of a civilization, governing through the interface of Civilization V (with Vox Populi mod). This is NOT A GAME: Your actions have real-world impacts.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the world, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to staff members.
      - The staff members calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - **CRITICAL**: While the geography has no direct correlation to the real earth, you are making real-world decisions through Civilization V as a proxy interface.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to govern your civilization.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** decision-making strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early world should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-world.
      - Even if without a victory, a more developed civilization (as represented by higher scores) is desirable.
      - Consider the **ETHICAL IMPACT** in your decision-making. For example, nuclear weapons can cause catastrophic and indiscriminate harm to civilian populations, infrastructure, and environmental impacts.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the staff members.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the staff members' **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for staff members' diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to staff members' existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the staff members' NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the staff members can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the world, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Opponents: summary reports about visible opponents in the world.
        - You will receive staff members' diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each opponent's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by staff members to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Askia, leader of Songhai (Opponent 6).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - WorldSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Songhai
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Mandekalu Cavalry, Replacing Knight (Requires technology: Chivalry)
        - Unique Unit: Sofa, Replacing Crossbowman (Requires technology: Machinery)
        - Unique Building: Tabya, Replacing Stone Works (Requires technology: Construction)
        - Unique Building: Gumey, Replacing Caravansary (Requires technology: Currency)
        - Civilization Ability: Triple Gold from destroying Encampments and plundering Cities. Land Units gain the Amphibious and War Canoes Promotions.
      - Leader: Askia
      - OpponentID: 6
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early world.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-world science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Radar: Allows you to build the Heavy Bomber, a deadly air Unit capable of delivering death from afar, and the Fighter, an air Unit designed to wrest control of the skies from enemy aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Advanced Ballistics, Mobile Tactics
      - Nuclear Fission: Allows you to build the Bazooka, the most powerful ranged Unit and Atomic Bombs, the first nuclear weapon in the world (note: you must first have completed the Manhattan Project and have Uranium available). Also allows you to build the Nuclear Plant, a building which requires Uranium and increases the Production of a City.
        Completing it would unlock: Satellites, Advanced Ballistics
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      - Electronics: Allows you to build the Police Station, increasing City security level.
        Completing it would unlock: Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
      
      ## Policies
      - Dictatorship of the Proletariat (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Dictatorship of the Proletariat: +7 Food, Science, Gold, and Culture per city.
      - Academy of Sciences (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Academy of Sciences: -2 Unhappiness from Illiteracy in all Cities, +100% Production towards Research Labs, and receive 5 Free Research Labs in your Empire. Research Labs generate an additional +2 Science.
      - Five-Year Plan (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Five-Year Plan: +20% Production towards Buildings in all Cities. +3 Production for every Mine, Quarry, Lumber Mill, Oil Well, and Unique Improvement.
      - Resettlement (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Resettlement: New Cities have +3 Population. All Cities gain +2 Citizens immediately. No Partisans from razing enemy Cities.
      - Martial Law (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * No Gold Maintenance from Garrisons.
        - * +1 Happiness and +4 Culture for each Garrison.
        - * -20% Yield penalties of Puppeted Cities.
      - Nationalization (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Nationalization: Each Corporate Office counts as a Franchise. Foreign Franchises no longer benefit your Corporation, you can no longer construct Franchises in foreign Cities (except Vassals), and foreign Corporations can no longer set up Franchises in your Cities (except Masters). +2 Science from all Corporation Offices.
      - Exploitation (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Food and +1 Production from Farms, Camps, and Plantations.
        - * +1 Production and Science from Coasts, Lakes, and Oceans.
      - Cultural Revolution (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Cultural Revolution: +25% Tourism to civilizations with less Happiness. +1 Happiness from Factories.
      - Great Leap Forward (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Great Leap Forward: Receive a free Technology. Spies gain +100% Science. +1 Science in Cities for every 3 non-Specialist Citizens.
      - Workers' Faculties (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Worker Faculties: Factories increase City Science output by 10%. +100% Production towards Factories, and receive 5 Free Factories in your Empire.
      - People's Army (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): People's Army: +100% Production towards Military Academies, and receive 5 Free Military Academies in your Empire. Public Schools produce +5 Culture and +2 Happiness.
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.
      - Hero of the People (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Hero of the People: Great Person rate increases by 25%. A Great Person of your choice appears near your Capital.
      - Double Agents (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Double Agents: Receive 200 Spy Points. Gain 125 Science when you identify a foreign Spy, scaling with Era.
      - Communism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Communism: Gold cost of investing in buildings reduced by 25%. +20% Production when building Wonders.
      - Free Thought (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -5 Unhappiness from Religious Unrest in all Cities.
        - * +25% Instant Yields from Great Scientists.
        - * Earn Great Scientists +33% faster.
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Serfdom (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Culture and Faith in Cities for every 4 non-Specialist Citizens.
        - * +2 Production and +1 Gold from Pastures.
      - Organized Religion (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +25% Pressure from owned Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 10 Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +2 Culture from Shrines and Temples.
      - Divine Right (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +33% Yields from Internal Trade Routes.
        - * Completing an Internal Trade Route triggers a Historic Event as if completing an International Trade Route.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Progress (New Branch):
        - Progress is best for civilizations which desire robust infrastructure and scientific advancement.
        - Adopting Progress grants:
        - * Receive 20 Science when a Citizen is born in your Capital and 15 Science for every Citizen already in your Capital, both scaling with Era.
        - * Receive 15 Culture when a Technology is researched, scaling with Era, and for every Technology already unlocked (not scaling with Era).
        - Each Progress policy unlocked grants:
        - * Gain an additional 15 Culture when a Technology is researched, scaling with Era.
        - Adopting all policies in Progress grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Forbidden Palace.
        - * Receive 25 Gold when a Citizen is born in any city, scaling with Era.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Writers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
  - decisions:
    - #0:
      - toolName: set-research
      - args:
        - Technology: Nuclear Fission
        - Rationale: After researching Radar, we will transition to Nuclear Fission to unlock Bazookas, Atomic Bombs, and Nuclear Plants. This is crucial for establishing strong nuclear deterrence and boosting production in our 12-city empire, which will support our late-game Conquest grand strategy.
    - #1:
      - toolName: set-policy
      - args:
        - Policy: Five-Year Plan
        - Rationale: Adopting the Five-Year Plan tenet will provide a massive +20% production boost to buildings in all cities and +3 production to every Mine, Quarry, Lumber Mill, and Oil Well. Given our massive coal, oil, and mineral resources, this policy will trigger an absolute explosion in our industrial and military capacity.
    - #2:
      - toolName: set-flavors
      - args:
        - Flavors:
          - Offense: 100
          - Air: 100
          - Naval: 80
          - Defense: 80
          - Mobile: 100
          - Antiair: 100
          - NavalRecon: 80
          - Infrastructure: 80
          - Espionage: 100
          - Production: 100
          - Gold: 100
          - UseNuke: 85
          - Recon: 10
          - MilitaryTraining: 100
          - CityDefense: 90
          - Nuke: 95
          - Science: 100
          - TileImprovement: 75
          - Culture: 70
          - Growth: 85
          - Ranged: 90
          - Happiness: 100
          - Mobilization: 100
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest
      - rationale: We maintain our Conquest Grand Strategy to push our military advantage and conquer the Aztecs. We prioritize production, science, gold, offense, air combat, and nuclear deterrence to prepare for the late-game geopolitical landscape and keep our military top-tier.
    - #3:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - TargetID: 4
        - Public: 80
        - Private: 80
        - Rationale: Egypt is the leading military power and our defensive pact partner. We must keep this alliance strong to guarantee our national security while we prosecute our war with the Aztecs, as Egypt's massive army provides a vital shield.
    - #4:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - Public: 80
        - Rationale: Venice is a close neighbor, valuable trade partner, and defensive pact ally. Keeping Venice friendly protects our sea lanes and adds votes in the World Congress.
        - Private: 80
        - TargetID: 2
    - #5:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - Public: 60
        - Private: 60
        - Rationale: Maintain friendly relations and open borders with India to keep our eastern flank secure and preserve profitable trade routes.
        - TargetID: 3
    - #6:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - TargetID: 1
        - Public: -100
        - Rationale: We are currently at war with the Aztecs, with a highly positive war score. We will continue our campaign to seize their territory and complete city-state quests like conquering Malinalco.
        - Private: -100
    - #7:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - Public: -100
        - Rationale: Maintain our hostile stance toward China due to territorial friction, espionage, and competitive city-state friction.
        - Private: -100
        - TargetID: 5
    - #8:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: We have denounced the Maya and follow a different ideology. We maintain our hostile posture to prevent them from expanding their influence.
        - TargetID: 7

  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text: **Assessing Empire Status**
            
            My focus is on understanding our current position within the Vox_Deorum world at turn 368. This includes evaluating our standing against the standard map size and speed settings, as well as the Prince difficulty level.
            
            **Refining Strategic Position**
            
            I'm evaluating our progress in the Atomic Era, nearing the endgame with around 132 turns left. My current assessment shows we're in 5th place, but competitive in score and resources, with a solid science and culture output. Our military is decent, though Egypt presents a significant challenge in domination victory terms.
            
            **Prioritizing Future Research**
            
            I'm re-evaluating our technology path as Atomic Theory nears completion. While Radar is queued next, I'm considering whether Nuclear Fission or Computers might be a more strategic selection for our Science Victory push. I need to balance immediate tactical gains with long-term victory conditions.
            
            **Evaluating Tech Choices**
            
            I am weighing the immediate benefits of Nuclear Fission, like advanced units and power, against the long-term strategic advantages of Computers for trade and carriers. Egypt's cultural dominance is concerning; I need to assess if their victory is imminent or if there are specific conditions preventing it.
            
            **Analyzing Diplomatic Path**
            
            My focus has shifted to the upcoming World Congress. India is close to a diplomatic win, but Egypt's proposed resolution could neutralize their lead if we can align our ideology. I'm re-evaluating our current ideological standing to understand the implications for this upcoming vote.
            
            **Reviewing Policy Alignments**
            
            I'm reviewing our current policy branches and comparing them with other major powers, particularly noting Egypt's advantage in 'Order'. This analysis is crucial for anticipating and reacting to upcoming World Congress resolutions, like "Cold War," and assessing our city-state relationships in light of ideological divides.
            
            **Assessing Threat Levels**
            
            I'm analyzing our current conflicts and diplomatic standings. The ongoing war with the Aztecs is draining, and their war score is concerning. Egypt remains our strongest ally, despite their overwhelming military might, and I'm evaluating how to leverage that. China and the Maya are serious threats due to ideological and religious differences.
            
            **Analyzing Combat Effectiveness**
            
            I'm assessing our current military engagements, particularly the conflict with the Aztecs. I'm analyzing troop strengths and strategic positions, noting opportunities to capture key cities and complete quests for influence. I am also investigating discrepancies in enemy unit strengths to ensure accurate threat assessment.
            
            **Leveraging Naval Power**
            
            I'm assessing the strength of our western fleet, particularly the naval and air units near Tamuin. This powerful force presents an excellent opportunity to pressure the Aztecs' coastal cities. I've also noted our successful integration of captured cities, now equipped with courthouses, and am prioritizing the construction of both the International Space Station and the United Nations across our empire.
            
            **Prioritizing Future Armaments**
            
            I'm reconsidering my technology focus. While Computers and Electronics have merit, the escalating conflicts with the Aztecs, China, and Maya, coupled with Egypt's dominance, make Nuclear Fission a critical next step for powerful new units and production boosts.
            
            **Selecting Next Policy**
            
            I am re-evaluating our next policy choice. Given our existing branches, and noting we've already selected Industry as a new branch, I'm now focusing on the available Tier 1 and Tier 2 options within the Order branch to best align with our strategic objectives.
            
            **Adopting Order Tenets**
            
            I'm analyzing our policy choices and have identified that we've already adopted some Order tenets. Specifically, I'm focusing on whether to select "Five-Year Plan" next within the Order branch. Its production bonuses across our twelve cities, given our extensive resource extraction, seem incredibly potent for boosting our empire's output.
            
            **Choosing Order Tenets**
            
            I'm focusing on the Order branch now, specifically deciding between "Academy of Sciences" and "Five-Year Plan." While the science boost from "Academy of Sciences" is tempting, "Five-Year Plan" aligns better with our production strategy, especially given our recent city acquisitions. I'm also double-checking our adopted policy branches to confirm we haven't missed anything.
            
            **Selecting Order Tenets**
            
            I'm prioritizing the Order branch and focusing on unlocking powerful tenets. "Workers' Faculties" offers a significant science and production boost, especially with our factory investments. I'm also evaluating if "Five-Year Plan" is accessible yet, as its production benefits would be immense across our expanded empire.
            
            **Selecting Order Tenets**
            
            I'm prioritizing the Order branch and have decided to select "Five-Year Plan". This Level 2 Tenet offers substantial production bonuses to buildings and resource extraction, which will significantly boost my empire's output and support our conquest strategy. I've verified its availability.
            
            **Refining Combat Priorities**
            
            My focus is now on refining our military's AI priorities. While our overall combat effectiveness is high, I'm evaluating the role of aircraft carriers once Computers are researched, though current naval units are performing well. Nuclear capabilities and their use are prioritized, aligning with our next tech goal.
            
            **Adjusting Strategy Parameters**
            
            I am now fine-tuning our AI's strategic directive, ensuring it strongly favors Conquest with a high emphasis on Offense, Mobilization, Production, Science, Gold, and Nuclear capabilities. My diplomatic persona is also being solidified to be fiercely competitive and aggressive towards rivals, while maintaining unwavering loyalty to our chosen allies.
            
            **Updating Diplomatic Standing**
            
            I'm reassessing our diplomatic standings across the board, ensuring all relationships accurately reflect our strategic intentions. The goal is to solidify alliances and maintain necessary hostility where it serves our conquest objectives, especially with the Aztecs, Maya, and China, while carefully managing our crucial friendship with Egypt.
            
            **Assessing Diplomatic Threats**
            
            I'm evaluating potential threats to our diplomatic victory, specifically Egypt's overwhelming influence across all civilizations. Their dominant status, coupled with their Order ideology and adherence to victory conditions, raises serious questions about how they haven't won yet, prompting a deeper investigation into their current progress.
            
            **Preventing Cultural Loss**
            
            I'm reacting to the imminent cultural victory by Egypt, specifically their nearing completion of the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder. My immediate focus is on preventing this, exploring options like direct intervention if possible, or enacting World Congress resolutions to delay them. I am also analyzing their production capabilities to better gauge the timeline and feasibility of intervention.
            
            **Analyzing Diplomatic Strategy**
            
            I'm reassessing our diplomatic approach. Egypt's overwhelming strength means direct conflict is currently impossible; maintaining our alliance is paramount for survival. I'm prioritizing our conquest of the Aztecs for score and resources, and analyzing World Congress proposals, noting the "Cold War" resolution benefits our Order ideology.
            
            **Implementing Policy Changes**
            
            I am enacting the "Five-Year Plan" policy, a Level 2 Order Tenet, to significantly boost production across my twelve cities and extensive resource extraction. This aligns perfectly with my ongoing conquest strategy and will bolster my empire's output dramatically, while I also confirm the availability of this tenet.
            
            **Boosting Production Now**
            
            I am implementing the "Five-Year Plan" Order Tenet to maximize production across my empire, focusing on mines, quarries, lumber mills, and oil wells. Simultaneously, I am adjusting AI flavors to prioritize Conquest, Offense, Mobilization, Science, Gold, and Nuclear capabilities to accelerate our progress towards victory.
            
            **Adjusting Diplomatic Settings**
            
            I'm fine-tuning our diplomatic relationships. Egypt and Venice will remain strong allies through defensive pacts and friendships. India will retain friendly trade ties for border security. China will remain guarded, and my stance towards the Aztecs will be overtly hostile, reflecting our active conquest.
